<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For the ordinary nearest-neighbor XY model on the square lattice, we prove the Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless prediction that the critical spin-correlation exponent is 1/4. More precisely, at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature, the infinite-volume correlation is $`n^{-1/4+o(1)}`$. The infinite-volume limit through free square boxes is taken before the separation limit.

- **claim-critical-spin-exponent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the model <a href="#eq:xy-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:xy-law">[eq:xy-law]</a> and the threshold <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>, $$\lim_{\substack{n\to\infty\\n\in\mathbb N,\ n\ge2}} \frac{\log C_{b_c}(n)}{\log n}=-\frac14.$$ Equivalently, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $n_0<\infty$ such that $$n^{-1/4-\varepsilon}\le C_{b_c}(n)\le n^{-1/4+\varepsilon} \qquad(n\ge n_0).$$
  - Explanation: The mass-defined critical inverse temperature has spin-correlation exponent 1/4 for the nearest-neighbor cosine model on the square lattice. The conclusion uses the free-box limit before the separation limit and permits multiplicative logarithmic corrections.
- **claim-critical-polynomial-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-critical-lower)): For every $n\ge1$, $$C_{b_c}(n)\ge\frac1{8n}.$$
  - Explanation: An established nonsharp lower bound prevents critical correlations from having arbitrarily fast polynomial decay. It supplies the endpoint input used to exclude a vanishing critical height coefficient.
- **claim-free-boundary-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-duality)): In a finite free spin rectangle, the two-point spin correlation is the ratio of a twisted height partition sum to the centered height partition sum, both with weights $p_b$ and exterior face height zero. The twist is an integral connection with circulations $+2\pi,-2\pi$ around the two source vertices and zero circulation elsewhere. For interior sources it can vanish near the outer boundary. After cuts, the same description holds on each retained chart, without identifying heights through the cut.
  - Explanation: The spin correlation equals a twisted-to-centered dual-height partition ratio. The exterior face is fixed at zero, and cuts retain separate height charts rather than identifying their traces.
- **claim-finite-comparison-rules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparisons)): On a fixed primary graph, allow additional nonnegative quadratic forms in real linear observations, centered equalities, and independent Gaussian observation noises.* 1. *Increasing centered precision decreases centered covariance and every centered real moment generating function. Covariance on a translate, including after a real linear tilt, dominates centered covariance. In particular $$\log\mathbb Ee^X\ge \tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)$$ for a centered inversion-symmetric law and a real linear test $X$.* 2. *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same positive precision is added to the affine and centered coordinates. Shifts may be nonzero equality values, an integral connection, or centers of observation penalties. The monotonicity also holds with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent, but the bound by one need not hold for this tilted ratio. Equality constraints may be inserted by infinite penalties whenever the affine constraint is nonempty.* 3. *Centered characteristic coefficients are nonnegative and increase with precision. Their logarithms have Hessian at every phase at least the Hessian at zero. A phase on a free component is allowed if its coefficient total is an integer. In the invariant finite-graph situations used below these coefficients are positive.* *Released constant modes are handled modulo translations or by confining observations. Real tests used when releasing a constant annihilate that constant.
  - Explanation: Centered precision controls covariance, real transforms, affine partition ratios, and characteristic coefficients. Numerator-only real tilts preserve ratio monotonicity but do not preserve the bound by one.
- **claim-primary-reference-cost-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-cost)): Suppose a primary test has a flow representation $$X=\sum_e w_e\nabla_e h,\qquad \mathcal R(w)=\sum_e w_e^2.$$ In a centered law with any additional centered precision, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)\le C_b\mathcal R(w),\qquad \mathbb E\|X\|^r\le C_{r,b}\mathcal R(w)^{r/2}. \label{eq:reference-moments}$$ For each fixed $t$, $\mathbb E\exp(tX/\sqrt{\mathcal R(w)})$ is bounded uniformly in the graph and in sufficiently fine subdivision. The constants can be uniform for $b$ in a compact subset of $(0,\infty)$. These statements include minimization over flows with the prescribed divergence and grounded vertices.
  - Explanation: Primary unit-edge flow energy bounds centered variance and polynomial moments, and controls normalized exponential transforms uniformly through subdivision. The bound concerns whole-chain increments rather than the inverse microscopic Gaussian precision.
- **claim-free-height-gaussian-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-free-limits)): There exists a finite $a=a(b)\ge0$ such that $a_m(b)\to a$. Let $B_n$, at mesh $n^{-1}$, be any sequence of free axis rectangles with endpoints converging to those of a nondegenerate rectangle $B$. For every finite collection of smooth shifts defined near $\overline B$, $$\log\mathbb E\exp\{\langle h,A_{B_n}g(\cdot/n)\rangle\} \longrightarrow \frac a2\int_B\|\nabla g\|^2. \label{eq:free-gradient-limit}$$ Joint limits are Gaussian, with covariance $a\int_B\nabla g\cdot\nabla g'$, and all joint moments converge. For zero-total profiles $f_n$ whose scaled densities converge in $L^2(B)$ to a bounded piecewise continuous $f$ of integral zero, $$\log\mathbb Ee^{\langle h,f_n\rangle} \longrightarrow \frac a2\langle f,(-\Delta_{B,N})^{-1}f\rangle_{L^2(B)}. \label{eq:free-smear-limit}$$ Face smears, after subtracting their totals from a volume smear, are allowed as well. These limits may be degenerate when $a=0$.
  - Explanation: Free rectangular height laws have a finite effective coefficient and Gaussian limits for smooth gradient observations and admissible mean-free smears. The limiting field can be degenerate.
- **claim-height-coefficient-gap** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-coefficient-gap)): For every $b>0$, $$a(b)=0\quad\hbox{or}\quad a(b)\ge8\pi.$$ If $a(b)>0$, the fractional mean $\langle h,u_m\rangle\bmod2\pi$ converges to uniform measure on the circle, independently of any fixed finite collection of the Gaussian observations of Theorem <a href="#thm:free-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:free-limits">6</a>. More generally, for a fixed nonzero integer $l$ and any zero-total profiles $t_m$ of bounded reference cost, $$w_m(lu_m+t_m)\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:uniform-phase}$$
  - Explanation: The height coefficient either vanishes or is at least the universal marginal value. In the positive regime, fractional means become uniform on the circle independently of fixed Gaussian observations, and bounded-cost mean-free perturbations do not preserve nonzero integer charge coefficients.
- **claim-spin-upper-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-spin-upper)): For the prescribed free-box correlation, $$\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log C_b(n)}{\log n} \le-\frac{2\pi}{a(b)}. \label{eq:spin-upper}$$ When $a(b)=0$, the assertion means that the limsup is $-\infty$, or equivalently that every fixed polynomial upper exponent is eventually available.
  - Explanation: The positive height coefficient gives an upper bound on the logarithmic spin-correlation exponent. A vanishing coefficient instead gives eventual upper bounds of every fixed polynomial order.
- **claim-mixed-height-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-mixed-limits)): For every fixed geometry above and every $a(b)>0$, the massless height law with primary hard pins has Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for fixed bounded, piecewise continuous density smears. On a component meeting a pin, the covariance is the inverse of $$a^{-1}\mathcal E_U \quad\hbox{on }\mathcal H_{U,p}.$$ On an unpinned component this assertion concerns mean-free smears. Its fractional mean modulo $2\pi$ is asymptotically uniform and independent of the mean-free Gaussian field.* *Adding a fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian law. If the penalty controls every unpinned component constant, lattice translations are summed and the limiting constants are integrated; the result is an ordinary, unprojected Gaussian law. The assertions include energy-approximable shifts and hold along every admissible primary mesh sequence.
  - Explanation: Primary hard pins produce the appropriate mixed Gaussian inverse form. Mean-free observations on unpinned components retain an independent uniform fractional mean; confining average penalties instead integrate the component constants.
- **claim-separated-pin-conditional-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pin-interaction)): Under the centered high law, $D_n(H,p)\ge0$ converges to the Gaussian pin interaction for precision $$a^{-1}\mathcal E_U(v)+\lVert Bv\rVert^2.$$ For every fixed finite list of density or box smears $X$, the conditional Laplace transforms and conditional weak laws given $h_p$ have the Gaussian finite-observation approximations just defined. Moreover, $$0<r_{H,n}\le1,\qquad \mathbb E_{\mu_n}r_{H,n}=e^{-D_n(H,p)},$$ and $r_{H,n}$ has the corresponding Gaussian approximation, whose limit is strictly positive almost surely. The convergence assertions persist when data are sampled under a fixed finite linear exponential tilt, with $D_n$ and $r_{H,n}$ retaining their centered definitions and the limiting law sampled under the corresponding Gaussian tilt. The displayed expectation identity concerns $\mu_n$, not its tilts. Conditional transforms for a tilted baseline also converge, by taking ratios of the centered conditional transforms.
  - Explanation: Separated hard-pin interactions and conditional laws converge through finite-observation approximations at typical guard data. The normalized core-pin ratio stays positive and at most one; its expectation identity belongs to the centered high law.
- **claim-band-density-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pins-band-density)): For every fixed $\lambda>0$, $q\ge0$, and $1\le p<\infty$, $$\limsup_{\ell\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathcal P_n\bigl(\widehat W^p(1+\|S\|)^q\bigr)<\infty, \qquad S=(h,u), \label{eq:pins-density-moments}$$ where $u$ is the uniform primary probability on $U$. In the first two limits of <a href="#eq:pins-limit-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pins-limit-order">[eq:pins-limit-order]</a>, fixed density smears under the band law converge with every fixed moment to the Gaussian field of precision $$T_\lambda=-\Delta_U/a+\lambda1_K.$$ For a smooth shift $g$ vanishing near $K$, its weak Neumann Laplacian test has limiting variance $a\mathcal E_U(g)$, including its free-face terms. Its limiting fourth moment is bounded uniformly for $\lambda\ge\lambda_0>0$.
  - Explanation: Normalized band weights have polynomially weighted moment bounds in the stated iterated limits. Fixed density observations converge to a Gaussian law with band mass, while shifts avoiding the band retain their gradient-energy variance.
- **claim-primary-path-anchoring** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pins-path)): After exposure of a cluster of scaled diameter at least $r>0$, the centered posterior moments of every fixed primary density smear are bounded in terms of its unit-edge reference cost, uniformly in subdivision. The remaining full-component mean has reference cost at most $V_r$. If $\mathcal D$ consists of band cells touched by the exposed path, and $\ell<r/C$, the reference cost of $\sum_{d\in\mathcal D}\|d\|b_dh_d^U$ is at most $$C\ell^2\sum_{d\in\mathcal D}\|d\|b_d^2. \label{eq:pins-local-path}$$ Here averages retain the full primary cell denominator. The same conclusions hold for exploration from an entire filled starting region. A probability average in a cell reached by a path from a fixed multiple of its diameter away has a uniformly bounded posterior reference cost.
  - Explanation: An exposed macroscopic cluster anchors centered posterior primary observations with subdivision-uniform flow costs. Touched-cell estimates retain the full primary averaging denominator and extend to exploration from a filled starting region.
- **claim-polynomial-cluster-tail-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pins-tails)): For $t\ge1$ and every integer $p\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned} \nu_n(Q\le4t^2)&\le C(1+t),\notag\\ \int_{Q\le4t^2} \left(\sum_CM_C(f)^2\right)^p\,\mathrm d\nu_n &\le C_p(1+t) \left(t\left\|\sum_xf_x\right\| +\mathcal R(f-u\textstyle\sum_xf_x)^{1/2}\right)^{2p}. \label{eq:pins-bin} \end{aligned}$$ After exposure of a cluster of diameter at least $r$, the complement squared-mass sum $Q_U$ has conditional moments of every fixed order bounded uniformly by constants depending on that order and $r$. Consequently, for every nonnegative intrinsic completed-cluster cost $I(C)$, and all sufficiently large $t=t(r)$, $$\int\sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}C\ge r}I(C)1_{\{m_C\le2t\}}\,\mathrm d\nu_n \le 2\int1_{\{Q\le8t^2\}} \sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}C\ge r}I(C)1_{\{m_C\le2t\}}\,\mathrm d\nu_n. \label{eq:pins-reinsertion}$$ For every $j>0$ there is a sufficiently large $M$ such that $$\mathcal P_n\left((1+\|S\|)^{-M} 1_{\{t^2<Q\le4t^2\}}\right) \le C_j(1+t)^{-j}. \label{eq:pins-damped-tail}$$ All estimates are uniform in large subdivision and fine primary mesh on the fixed geometry.
  - Explanation: Signless cluster-mass bins, completed-cluster reinsertion, and polynomial damping provide uniform tail control. These estimates avoid both microscopic cluster counting and a Gaussian tail assumption for Bessel heights.
- **claim-weighted-cluster-moment-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pins-weighted)): At fixed $\lambda$, all fixed moments of squared primary cluster-mass sums and signlessly selected signed partial sums are bounded in the first two limits of <a href="#eq:pins-limit-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pins-limit-order">[eq:pins-limit-order]</a>. For a band cell $d$, with $D_\ell^2=1+\log(1/\ell)$, $$\mathbb E_W\left(\sum_CM_C(d)^2\right)^p +\mathbb E_W\|d_*\|^{2p} \le C_{p,\lambda}D_\ell^{2p}+o_n(1). \label{eq:pins-cell-moments}$$ Here $d_*$ is any signed selection determined by signless data, with nonnegative coefficients bounded by the full cell smear. Fixed observation boxes have a fixed constant in place of $D_\ell$.
  - Explanation: Polynomial cluster bounds transfer to the band-weighted law, including signless selections of signed partial sums. Cell observations carry a logarithmic reference-cost scale, whereas fixed observation boxes have a fixed bound.
- **claim-macroscopic-fair-sign-resampling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pins-resampling)): At fixed $r,\lambda>0$, replacing the signs of all clusters of diameter at least $r$ by independent fair signs changes the band law by total variation tending to zero as $n\to\infty$ and then $\ell\downarrow0$. Clusters of diameter below $r$ contribute vanishing fixed moments to bounded density smears as $r\downarrow0$ after these limits. For nonnegative fixed density smears $f,g$, $$\limsup_{\ell\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb E_W\sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}C\ge r}M_C(f)M_C(g) \le (f,T_\lambda^{-1}g). \label{eq:pins-cross}$$ Every fixed moment of $\sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}C\ge r}M_C(f)^2$ has a limiting upper bound uniform for $\lambda\ge\lambda_0>0$.
  - Explanation: Macroscopic signs can be resampled fairly with vanishing total-variation error after the ordered refinements. Small clusters disappear from bounded density observations, and Gaussian covariance bounds the surviving nonnegative cross-mass sums.
- **claim-vanishing-band-reach** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-pins-reach)): Under the band law, the probability that an exploration starting in a fixed positive-area region on one side of a separating positive-width band reaches a fixed closed region on the other side tends to zero in <a href="#eq:pins-limit-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pins-limit-order">[eq:pins-limit-order]</a>. The assertion includes exploration from a filled exterior or from a primary hard-pin region before imposing its pin.
  - Explanation: A separating positive-width band suppresses exploration from a positive-area starting region to the opposite target in the prescribed order of mesh, cell, and strength limits. Filled exterior and pre-pin starting regions are included.
- **claim-restoration-two-way-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restoration)): Let $\mu_n$ be a cut centered high law and let $\nu_n$ restore bonds or positive forms supported inside a core $H$, separated from the guard $p$. Include both primary endpoints of every restored bond, and the support of every restored form, in the hard core pin. If $Q_\Delta$ is the positive restoration factor and $$r_\Delta(b^p)= \frac{\mathbb E_{\mu_n}(Q_\Delta\mid h_p=b^p)} {\mathbb E_{\mu_n}(Q_\Delta\mid h_p=0)},$$ then $$r_{H,n}\le r_\Delta\le1,\qquad \frac{\,\mathrm d\nu_{n,p}}{\,\mathrm d\mu_{n,p}} =\frac{r_\Delta}{\mathbb E_{\mu_n}r_\Delta},\qquad r_{H,n}\le\frac{\,\mathrm d\nu_{n,p}}{\,\mathrm d\mu_{n,p}} \le e^{D_n(H,p)}. \label{eq:pins-restoration}$$ Events concerning random guard data whose probabilities tend to zero transfer in both directions between the two topologies. This typical-data transfer also holds when either law is sampled with a fixed finite linear tilt. The displayed identities and inequalities remain statements about the centered laws and their centered-defined ratios.
  - Explanation: Restoring bonds or positive forms entirely inside the pinned core bounds the centered guard density between the core-pin ratio and the interaction exponential. Vanishing-probability events transfer in both directions at typical data, including fixed linear-tilt sampling.
- **claim-spin-magnetic-lower-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-spin-lower)): For the prescribed free-box correlation, $$\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log C_b(n)}{\log n} \ge -\frac{2\pi}{a}.$$ In particular, $m(b)=0$.
  - Explanation: A positive height coefficient gives the lower logarithmic spin-correlation exponent matching the upper bound and therefore implies zero horizontal spin mass.
- **claim-many-scale-sector-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sector-comparison)): For the preceding geometry there are numbers $\eta_R\to0$ such that, for each sufficiently large fixed $R$, one can choose a fixed $m_*$ for which $$\label{eq:magnetic-sector-comparison} \left\|\log\frac{Z^s}{Z^0} -\log\frac{Z_{\mathrm g}^s}{Z_{\mathrm g}^0}\right\| \le C_M(1+\log n)\eta_R.$$ For these fixed choices, heights on the two inner rims, in the bounded cut coordinates just specified, are uniformly tight in $n$ and in the outer volume. This is absolute tightness, without subtracting an additive constant.
  - Explanation: Localized observation penalties allow comparison of discrete and Gaussian winding-sector partition ratios with a vanishing error per annular scale. Inner-rim heights remain absolutely tight with fixed hole and localization choices.
- **claim-isolation-pattern-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-magnetic-pattern)): For either model and either sector, after increasing $m_*$ at fixed sufficiently large $R$, $$\label{eq:magnetic-pattern} \|w(I)\|\le e^{-cRj}.$$ Here and below $c>0$ may decrease, and all fixed constants may depend on $b,a,M$, but not on $n$ or the outer volume.
  - Explanation: Each selected mark in the exact isolation expansion gains exponential suppression in the collar parameter. The estimate holds in both sectors and both models uniformly over separation and outer volume.
- **claim-supermarginal-openness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-openness)): If $a(b)>8\pi$, there is an open interval $I$ containing $b$, contained in $(0,\infty)$, such that $a(\widetilde b)>0$ for every $\widetilde b\in I$.
  - Explanation: A coefficient strictly above the marginal value implies positivity throughout a real parameter neighborhood. The proof does not assume continuity or differentiate the unknown coefficient.
- **claim-strengthened-local-gaussian-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-op-local-limits)): Fix a bounded mixed geometry in observation-cell units, with separated positive-area core and guard pins and observation masses controlling each unpinned constant. At the central parameter $b$, the strengthened laws have the Gaussian smear, conditional-smear, and pin-interaction limits of Theorems <a href="#thm:mixed-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:mixed-limits">9</a> and <a href="#thm:pin-interaction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:pin-interaction">10</a>, with coefficient $a'$, in the iterated limit $$\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{s_0\to\infty}.$$ The limits hold under each indicated pin set. The quantities $D$ and $r_H$ retain their centered-law definitions; finite-probe convergence of these conditional quantities holds also when their data are sampled under any fixed real observation tilt. This is convergence in probability under the corresponding guard laws, not a redefinition of the centered pin ratios. At fixed geometry the conclusions are uniform on compact sets of observation data.
  - Explanation: Added block-average precision yields Gaussian smear, conditional, and pin-interaction limits with the strengthened coefficient after ordered refinements. Pin ratios retain centered definitions when guard data are sampled under a fixed real tilt.
- **claim-observed-large-field-reserve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-op-large-field)): For every cut topology used in the entry construction, there is $c_*>0$, depending only on $\delta,a'$ and the fixed local geometry conventions, such that $$\label{eq:op-large-field} \frac{Z_G(y;\widetilde b)}{Z_G(0;\widetilde b)} \leq \exp\{-c_*E_G(y)/2\}.$$ The same constant works for all sufficiently large dyadic refinements and for $\widetilde b$ in a neighborhood of $b$.
  - Explanation: The added averaged precision supplies a refinement-independent Gaussian suppression of minimized observation energy. The bound is an observed-field estimate and does not assert a bare site-level Gaussian tail for the original Bessel law.
- **claim-strengthened-analytic-entry** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-op-entry)): For every $\epsilon>0$ there are fixed dyadic integers $q,R,t,s_0$, with $P=R^5$, $s=ts_0$, and $m=qRs$, and a real interval $I$ about $b$, such that the exact relative observation density of the strengthened law, after averaging the observation noise and reference modes below scale $m$, has plane and torus local subset activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\label{eq:op-entry-small} \lVert K_m(\widetilde b)\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V} \leq\epsilon\qquad(\widetilde b\in I).$$ The norm includes the full analytic field-derivative sum. The activities are real, even, square-covariant, invariant under block-grid translations and common field translations by $2\pi/\alpha$. The torus representation is exact for compatible sides $n\geq sP$, with exact plane copying on unwrapped padded supports and the copying property under subsequent local maps.
  - Explanation: The strengthened relative observation density admits exact local subset activities that are small in the full analytic norm throughout a parameter interval. The construction preserves the listed symmetries and exact compatible-torus copying.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-free-limits",
        "exact_quote": "There exists a finite $a=a(b)\\ge0$ such that $a_m(b)\\to a$. Let $B_n$, at mesh $n^{-1}$, be any sequence of free axis rectangles with endpoints converging to those of a nondegenerate rectangle $B$. For every finite collection of smooth shifts defined near $\\overline B$, $$\\log\\mathbb E\\exp\\{\\langle h,A_{B_n}g(\\cdot/n)\\rangle\\}\n \\longrightarrow \\frac a2\\int_B|\\nabla g|^2.\n \\label{eq:free-gradient-limit}$$ Joint limits are Gaussian, with covariance $a\\int_B\\nabla g\\cdot\\nabla g'$, and all joint moments converge. For zero-total profiles $f_n$ whose scaled densities converge in $L^2(B)$ to a bounded piecewise continuous $f$ of integral zero, $$\\log\\mathbb Ee^{\\langle h,f_n\\rangle}\n \\longrightarrow\n \\frac a2\\langle f,(-\\Delta_{B,N})^{-1}f\\rangle_{L^2(B)}.\n \\label{eq:free-smear-limit}$$ Face smears, after subtracting their totals from a volume smear, are allowed as well. These limits may be degenerate when $a=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-coefficient-gap",
        "exact_quote": "For every $b>0$, $$a(b)=0\\quad\\hbox{or}\\quad a(b)\\ge8\\pi.$$ If $a(b)>0$, the fractional mean $\\langle h,u_m\\rangle\\bmod2\\pi$ converges to uniform measure on the circle, independently of any fixed finite collection of the Gaussian observations of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:free-limits\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:free-limits\">6</a>.         "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-mixed-limits",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed geometry above and every $a(b)>0$, the massless height law with primary hard pins has Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for fixed bounded, piecewise continuous density smears. On a component meeting a pin, the covariance is the inverse of $$a^{-1}\\mathcal E_U\n                   \\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathcal H_{U,p}.$$ On an unpinned component this assertion concerns mean-free smears. Its fractional mean modulo $2\\pi$ is asymptotically uniform and independent of the mean-free Gaussian field.*\n\n*Adding a fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian law. If the penalty controls every unpinned component constant, lattice translations are summed and the limiting constants are integrated; the result is an ordinary, unprojected Gaussian law. The assertions include energy-approximable shifts and hold along every admissible primary mesh sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-pin-interaction",
        "exact_quote": "Under the centered high law, $D_n(H,p)\\ge0$ converges to the Gaussian pin interaction for precision $$a^{-1}\\mathcal E_U(v)+\\lVert Bv\\rVert^2.$$ For every fixed finite list of density or box smears $X$, the conditional Laplace transforms and conditional weak laws given $h_p$ have the Gaussian finite-observation approximations just defined. Moreover, $$0<r_{H,n}\\le1,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}r_{H,n}=e^{-D_n(H,p)},$$ and $r_{H,n}$ has the corresponding Gaussian approximation, whose limit is strictly positive almost surely. The convergence assertions persist when data are sampled under a fixed finite linear exponential tilt, with $D_n$ and $r_{H,n}$ retaining their centered definitions and the limiting law sampled under the corresponding Gaussian tilt. The displayed expectation identity concerns $\\mu_n$, not its tilts. Conditional transforms for a tilted baseline also converge, by taking ratios of the centered conditional transforms.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pins-band-density",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\lambda>0$, $q\\ge0$, and $1\\le p<\\infty$, $$\\limsup_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\mathcal P_n\\bigl(\\widehat W^p(1+|S|)^q\\bigr)<\\infty,\n \\qquad S=(h,u),\n \\label{eq:pins-density-moments}$$ where $u$ is the uniform primary probability on $U$. In the first two limits of <a href=\"#eq:pins-limit-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pins-limit-order\">[eq:pins-limit-order]</a>, fixed density smears under the band law converge with every fixed moment to the Gaussian field of precision $$T_\\lambda=-\\Delta_U/a+\\lambda1_K.$$ For a smooth shift $g$ vanishing near $K$, its weak Neumann Laplacian test has limiting variance $a\\mathcal E_U(g)$, including its free-face terms. Its limiting fourth moment is bounded uniformly for $\\lambda\\ge\\lambda_0>0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pins-path",
        "exact_quote": "After exposure of a cluster of scaled diameter at least $r>0$, the centered posterior moments of every fixed primary density smear are bounded in terms of its unit-edge reference cost, uniformly in subdivision. The remaining full-component mean has reference cost at most $V_r$. If $\\mathcal D$ consists of band cells touched by the exposed path, and $\\ell<r/C$, the reference cost of $\\sum_{d\\in\\mathcal D}|d|b_dh_d^U$ is at most $$C\\ell^2\\sum_{d\\in\\mathcal D}|d|b_d^2.\n \\label{eq:pins-local-path}$$ Here averages retain the full primary cell denominator. The same conclusions hold for exploration from an entire filled starting region. A probability average in a cell reached by a path from a fixed multiple of its diameter away has a uniformly bounded posterior reference cost.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },

        {

The same conclusions hold for exploration from an entire filled starting region.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pins-tails",
        "exact_quote": "For $t\\ge1$ and every integer $p\\ge1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\nu_n(Q\\le4t^2)&\\le C(1+t),\\notag\\\\\n \\int_{Q\\le4t^2}\n       \\left(\\sum_CM_C(f)^2\\right)^p\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_n\n &\\le C_p(1+t)\n \\left(t\\left|\\sum_xf_x\\right|\n       +\\mathcal R(f-u\\textstyle\\sum_xf_x)^{1/2}\\right)^{2p}.\n \\label{eq:pins-bin}\n\\end{aligned}$$ After exposure of a cluster of diameter at least $r$, the complement squared-mass sum $Q_U$ has conditional moments of every fixed order bounded uniformly by constants depending on that order and $r$. Consequently, for every nonnegative intrinsic completed-cluster cost $I(C)$, and all sufficiently large $t=t(r)$, $$\\int\\sum_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}C\\ge r}I(C)1_{\\{m_C\\le2t\\}}\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_n\n \\le\n 2\\int1_{\\{Q\\le8t^2\\}}\n       \\sum_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}C\\ge r}I(C)1_{\\{m_C\\le2t\\}}\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_n.\n \\label{eq:pins-reinsertion}$$ For every $j>0$ there is a sufficiently large $M$ such that $$\\mathcal P_n\\left((1+|S|)^{-M}\n                   1_{\\{t^2<Q\\le4t^2\\}}\\right)\n       \\le C_j(1+t)^{-j}.\n \\label{eq:pins-damped-tail}$$ All estimates are uniform in large subdivision and fine primary mesh on the fixed geometry.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pins-resampling",
        "exact_quote": "At fixed $r,\\lambda>0$, replacing the signs of all clusters of diameter at least $r$ by independent fair signs changes the band law by total variation tending to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ and then $\\ell\\downarrow0$. For nonnegative fixed density smears $f,g$, $$\\limsup_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\mathbb E_W\\sum_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}C\\ge r}M_C(f)M_C(g)\n       \\le (f,T_\\lambda^{-1}g).\n \\label{eq:pins-cross}$$ Every fixed moment of $\\sum_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}C\\ge r}M_C(f)^2$ has a limiting upper bound uniform for $\\lambda\\ge\\lambda_0>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-216.5"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-restoration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_n$ be a cut centered high law and let $\\nu_n$ restore bonds or positive forms supported inside a core $H$, separated from the guard $p$. Include both primary endpoints of every restored bond, and the support of every restored form, in the hard core pin. If $Q_\\Delta$ is the positive restoration factor and $$r_\\Delta(b^p)=\n \\frac{\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}(Q_\\Delta\\mid h_p=b^p)}\n      {\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}(Q_\\Delta\\mid h_p=0)},$$ then $$r_{H,n}\\le r_\\Delta\\le1,\\qquad\n \\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_{n,p}}{\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_{n,p}}\n   =\\frac{r_\\Delta}{\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}r_\\Delta},\\qquad\n r_{H,n}\\le\\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_{n,p}}{\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_{n,p}}\n                  \\le e^{D_n(H,p)}.\n \\label{eq:pins-restoration}$$ Events concerning random guard data whose probabilities tend to zero transfer in both directions between the two topologies. This typical-data transfer also holds when either law is sampled with a fixed finite linear tilt. The displayed identities and inequalities remain statements about the centered laws and their centered-defined ratios.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.5"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Free square-box XY law** ([source](#eq:xy-law)): At inverse temperature $b>0$, the angles $\theta_x\in\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z)$ have law $$\label{eq:xy-law} \,\mathrm d\mu_{L,b}(\theta) =Z_{L,b}^{-1} \exp\!\left\{b\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E_L} \cos(\theta_x-\theta_y)\right\} \prod_{x\in\Lambda_L}\frac{\,\mathrm d\theta_x}{2\pi}.$$
- **Infinite-volume correlation and horizontal mass** ([source](#eq:correlation)): With $e_1=(1,0)$, set $$\label{eq:correlation} C_b(n)=\lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\ge n}} \mathbb E_{\mu_{L,b}}\cos(\theta_0-\theta_{ne_1}),\qquad m(b)=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac1n\log C_b(n),$$
- **Mass-defined critical inverse temperature** ([source](#eq:critical-definition)): and $$\label{eq:critical-definition} b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}.$$
- **Primary Bessel edge weight** ([source](#sec:heights)): On an original, or *primary*, dual edge the weight of the difference $2\pi j$ is $$p_b(j)=e^{-b}I_j(b),\qquad \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}p_b(j)e^{tj}=\exp\{b(\cosh t-1)\}.$$
- **Primary unit-conductance Laplacian** ([source](#sec:heights)): For a retained primary graph, $A$ is its unit-conductance Laplacian: $$\langle g,Ag\rangle=\sum_{\{x,y\}}(g_x-g_y)^2.$$
- **Flow representation and reference cost** ([source](#stmt-lem-reference-cost)): Suppose a primary test has a flow representation $$X=\sum_e w_e\nabla_e h,\qquad \mathcal R(w)=\sum_e w_e^2.$$
- **Finite-box effective height coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $B_m=\{0,\ldots,m-1\}^2$, put $g_m(x)=x_1/m$ and $$a_m(b)=\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{B_m,b}\langle h,A_{B_m}g_m\rangle.$$
- **Mixed pinned energy space** ([source](#pm-h-12)): On a component $U$ with pin region $p$, its limiting energy space is $$\mathcal H_{U,p}=\{v\in H^1(U):v=0\text{ almost everywhere on }p\}, \qquad \mathcal E_U(v)=\int_U\|\nabla v\|^2.$$
- **Separated pin interaction and normalized pin ratio** ([source](#eq:pins-definitions)): For positively separated box regions $H,p$, initially without hard pins, put $$D_n(H,p)=\log \frac{\mu_n(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}{\mu_n(h_H=0)},\qquad r_{H,n}(b^p)= \frac{\mu_n(h_H=0\mid h_p=b^p)} {\mu_n(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}. \label{eq:pins-definitions}$$
- **Confining band weight** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Define $$W_{\ell,\lambda}(h)= \exp\left\{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_d\|d\|h_d^2\right\}. \label{eq:pins-band}$$
- **Finite-observation approximation at random pin data** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A function of $h_p$ has a Gaussian *finite-observation approximation* if, for every tolerance, it can be approximated in probability by a function of a sufficiently long fixed list of these probes, whose joint law and function converge to the Gaussian counterparts.
- **Strengthened reference coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Choose $\delta>0$ sufficiently small that $$\label{eq:op-reference} a'=(a^{-1}+\delta)^{-1}>8\pi,\qquad \alpha=\sqrt{a'}.$$

| q-critical-correlation-lower-bound | Critical spin correlation | ≥ | $\frac1{8n}$ | All positive integer separations at criticality. |
| q-positive-height-coefficient-gap | Positive effective height coefficient | ≥ | $8\pi$ | Every positive inverse temperature with a nonvanishing effective coefficient. |
| q-spin-upper-exponent | Upper logarithmic spin-correlation exponent | ≤ | $-\frac{2\pi}{a(b)}$ | Fixed inverse temperature; a vanishing coefficient is interpreted as arbitrarily fast polynomial decay. |
| q-spin-lower-exponent | Lower logarithmic spin-correlation exponent | ≥ | $-\frac{2\pi}{a}$ | Fixed positive height coefficient with localization parameters fixed before the separation limit. |
| q-primary-variance-reference-bound | Centered primary-test variance | ≤ | $C_b\mathcal R(w)$ | Fixed inverse temperature, uniformly in the graph and sufficiently fine subdivision. |
| q-band-gaussian-precision | Limiting band precision operator | = | $-\Delta_U/a+\lambda1_K$ | Primary mesh refinement followed by band-cell refinement at fixed band strength. |
| q-normalized-core-pin-ratio-upper-bound | Normalized conditional core-pin ratio | ≤ | $1$ | Centered-defined pin ratios; the centered expectation identity is not transferred to tilted sampling. |
| q-band-cell-logarithmic-cost-scale | Squared band-cell reference-cost scale | = | $1+\log(1/\ell)$ | The first two prescribed band limits at fixed strength. |
| q-isolation-pattern-suppression | Absolute normalized mark-pattern weight | ≤ | $e^{-cRj}$ | Fixed collar and microscopic-hole choices; constants are independent of separation and outer volume. |
| q-strengthened-reference-coefficient | Strengthened effective reference coefficient | = | $(a^{-1}+\delta)^{-1}$ | The added precision and Gaussian reference remain fixed as the physical inverse temperature varies. |
| q-observed-large-field-bound | Shifted-to-centered strengthened observation partition ratio | ≤ | $\exp\{-c_*E_G(y)/2\}$ | Sufficiently large dyadic refinements and a physical-parameter neighborhood. |
| q-critical-height-coefficient | Critical effective height coefficient | = | $8\pi$ | The free height coefficient at criticality. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-critical-external-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-critical-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-critical-lower): Match the external free-boundary cosine convention and identify its threshold through horizontal mass. -> Nonsharp critical polynomial lower bound.
- **step-planar-current-height-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-duality): Fourier-expand edge factors, integrate spin angles, rotate currents to dual edges, and subtract a fixed source path. -> Centered and twisted dual-height partition sums with exterior height zero.
- **step-gaussian-chain-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparisons): Replace primary edges by Gaussian chains, apply finite lattice comparisons on parallel affine supports, and take the fixed-primary-graph limit. -> Finite comparison rules for Bessel weights, including numerator-only real tilts.
- **step-whole-chain-reference-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-cost): Release endpoint, curl, and period constraints and bound normalized transforms using independent whole-chain increments. -> Subdivision-uniform primary variance, moment, and normalized exponential bounds.
- **step-free-coefficient-and-saturation** [claim](#stmt-thm-free-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-free-limits): Use cutting estimates to obtain the coefficient, reflection folding to saturate smooth energies, and small-cell Laplace expansion to identify Gaussian limits. -> Free Gaussian gradient and smear limits with joint moment convergence.
- **step-soft-joining-coefficient-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-coefficient-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-coefficient-gap): Softly join translation-summed cells, use the replica transform identity to force uniform fractional means, and compare radial Fourier sectors across scales. -> Uniform fractional phase and the forbidden positive coefficient range.
- **step-annular-spin-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-spin-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spin-upper): Insert tangential real tilts on disjoint annuli, release constraints into small cells, and optimize the limiting quadratic transform. -> Spin upper exponent, including the vanishing-coefficient interpretation.
- **step-band-density-limits** [claim](#stmt-lem-pins-band-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pins-band-density): Integrate the component constant after translation summation, control normalized weight moments, and pass cell penalties to the continuum inverse form. -> Band Gaussian limits and weighted integrability.
- **step-path-posterior-anchoring** [claim](#stmt-lem-pins-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pins-path): Zero-extend unvisited primary variations and apply face-connected path-Poincare inequalities before extending flows through chains. -> Uniform centered posterior reference costs after macroscopic exposure.
- **step-polynomial-bins-and-reinsertion** [claim](#stmt-lem-pins-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pins-tails): Combine fair-sign moment inequalities, translation-orbit counting, posterior complement moments, and polynomial damping. -> Cluster-mass bin, reinsertion, and damped-tail bounds.
- **step-transfer-weighted-cluster-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-pins-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pins-weighted): Cancel polynomial weights in Holder bounds and sum cluster-mass bins; estimate cell costs through the ordinary Neumann spectrum. -> Band-weighted cluster moments with the logarithmic cell-cost scale.
- **step-resample-macroscopic-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-pins-resampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pins-resampling): Bound changes in quadratic band energy on finite mass bins, use posterior path costs, and remove cutoffs with weighted moments. -> Asymptotically fair macroscopic signs and Gaussian cross-mass bounds.
- **step-screen-exploration-with-band** [claim](#stmt-prop-pins-reach) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pins-reach): Compare prior and reached-cell variances, control small and large posterior tilts separately, and force cross-band cluster mass to vanish. -> Vanishing exploration reach in the prescribed band limit order.
- **step-transfer-primary-hard-pin-limits** [claim](#stmt-thm-mixed-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pins-reach-2): Explore from the pin, squeeze posterior variances between band and hard-pin laws, and extend saturated shifts through reference-energy approximation. -> Mixed pinned Gaussian limits and confining finite-rank extensions.
- **step-pin-interaction-and-regression** [claim](#stmt-thm-pin-interaction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pins-reach-3): Use relative band pin estimates and partition-ratio cancellation for interaction convergence, then exhaust guard probes through Gaussian regression and core penalties. -> Conditional finite-observation limits and a strictly positive limiting normalized core-pin ratio.
- **step-local-restoration-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-restoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restoration): Represent restored bonds by independent chains and matching penalties, pin their primary supports, and apply Bayes' formula with positive limiting core-pin ratios. -> Centered guard density bounds and two-way typical-data transfer.
- **step-local-magnetic-pattern-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-magnetic-pattern) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-magnetic-pattern): Control centered normalization through local telescoping, cut beyond prescribed guards, and use conditional Gaussian localization at typical data. -> Exponential gain for separated magnetic marks.
- **step-sector-factorization-and-rim-tightness** [claim](#stmt-prop-sector-comparison) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Factor disconnected mark groups into a finite signed gas, prove positive deletion ratios, and add a distinguished rim insertion. -> Many-scale sector comparison and absolute inner-rim tightness.
- **step-fill-holes-and-spin-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-spin-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-magnetic-pattern-2): Fill fixed microscopic holes using positive edge weights, try the angular Gaussian target, and remove per-scale errors after the free-box and separation limits. -> Matching lower spin exponent and zero mass for a positive height coefficient.
- **step-strengthened-local-limit-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-op-local-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-op-local-limits): First integrate finite-rank average precision, then prove inverse-form and separated cross-determinant convergence as the subcell partition is refined. -> Strengthened local Gaussian and conditional limits with the fixed gapped reference.
- **step-average-precision-large-field-reserve** [claim](#stmt-lem-op-large-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-op-large-field): Complete the square on chain approximants, discard microscopic energy, and compare retained average energy with observation energy using adjacent-cell Poincare and interpolation. -> A refinement-independent large-field suppression for observed partition ratios.
- **step-full-analytic-entry** [claim](#stmt-lem-op-entry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-op-entry): Combine exact isolation and links, bounded-data conditional gains, original-face energy accounting, large-field reserve, and imaginary-direction interpolation before connected pattern summation. -> Small full analytic activities with exact plane and compatible-torus copying.
- **step-gapped-stability-and-original-variance** [claim](#stmt-prop-openness) / [proof](#pm-h-37): Run the fixed-reference analytic map in a small gapped tube, use a terminal torus variance diagnostic, and remove the added precision by centered comparison. -> Positivity of the original height coefficient throughout a real parameter neighborhood.
- **step-identify-critical-coefficient-and-exponent** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-op-entry-2): Exclude a vanishing coefficient using the critical lower bound, exclude a supermarginal coefficient using openness and the mass threshold, and squeeze the spin exponent. -> Exact marginal critical height coefficient and the critical spin-correlation exponent.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-critical-correlation-exponent**: At the mass-defined critical inverse temperature, the prescribed free-box correlation has the critical logarithmic decay exponent stated in the main theorem. The volume limit precedes the separation limit.
- **invariant-positive-coefficient-gap**: The effective height coefficient is either zero or lies above the stated universal lower threshold. Positivity also gives asymptotic uniformity of the fractional mean independently of the fixed Gaussian observations.
- **invariant-primary-hard-pin-limits**: Mixed Gaussian limits constrain primary heights in the pin regions and treat free components through mean-free smears and their fractional means. Confining observation penalties integrate the component constants after summing lattice translations.
- **invariant-centered-conditional-ratios**: Separated pin interactions and pin ratios retain their centered-law definitions when their data are sampled under a fixed linear tilt. Conditional convergence uses finite observations at typical guard data.
- **invariant-restoration-support**: Two-way transfer of negligible guard-data events requires the hard core pin to contain every restored bond endpoint and every restored form support.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-volume-certification**: Treat a finite-box correlation measurement as a certificate of the critical infinite-volume exponent. -> A finite box does not establish the prescribed volume limit or the subsequent asymptotic separation limit.
- **anti-sharp-amplitude-or-logarithmic-factor**: Extract a limiting critical amplitude or a specific multiplicative logarithmic correction from the critical exponent. -> The logarithmic decay exponent permits multiplicative corrections that the theorem does not identify.
- **anti-microscopic-gaussian-tail**: Use the bare inverse precision of a subdivided Gaussian chain as a uniform Gaussian tail bound for primary Bessel heights. -> Subdivision transfers finite comparison rules but does not supply the claimed uniform microscopic variance control.
- **anti-uniform-guard-data-limit**: Apply conditional Gaussian approximations uniformly to every prescribed microscopic guard configuration. -> Convergence at typical random data does not control arbitrary exceptional guard values.
- **anti-tilted-expectation-identity**: Use the centered pin-ratio expectation identity under tilted sampling without changing its expectation measure. -> The tilt changes the sampling law while the pin ratio retains its centered definition.
- **anti-smear-to-winding-inference**: Infer a winding-sector partition ratio directly from convergence of ordinary height observations. -> Ordinary observation limits do not determine the relative normalization of distinct winding sectors.
- **anti-changed-spin-boundary-model**: Apply the main critical correlation conclusion to a spin system with fixed boundary spins, fields, or boundary identifications. -> Those changes replace the spin law and boundary convention used by the duality and correlation limits.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result concerns an infinite-volume correlation followed by an unbounded separation limit. The supporting Gaussian, conditional-pin, sector-comparison, and analytic-entry results likewise require asymptotic refinements or unbounded height partition sums. The finite isolation expansion is a proof identity involving these partition sums, not a finite executable procedure deciding the theorem. Finite test vectors cannot certify these conclusions. The source explicitly states: "The infinite-volume limit through free square boxes is taken before the separation limit."

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The correlation theorem specifies an asymptotic mathematical result but supplies no executable implementation, finite stopping criterion, or software correctness contract. |

| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The strengthened-law entry controls an analytic activity norm, including field derivatives. It does not provide an engineering tool that computes or certifies that norm from finite input. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian limit theorem concerns convergence along refined rectangle sequences and permits a degenerate limit. It supplies no general algorithm for evaluating the effective coefficient with certified finite precision. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The primary graph, height coordinates, and Laplacian specify mathematical objects. Their definition does not construct a storage representation or establish operations and memory bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Subdivision is an auxiliary Gaussian-chain limit taken on a fixed primary graph. Uniform moment bounds through that limit provide no accelerator architecture, parallel execution contract, or hardware resource estimate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniformity of the fractional height mean is an asymptotic distributional statement under the specified height law. It does not construct a randomness extractor or establish adversarial security. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional laws are approximated through fixed smooth probes followed by probe exhaustion. This supplies a probabilistic limit theorem rather than a training procedure, prediction error bound, or learned model construction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The reference-cost construction minimizes squared flow energy with prescribed divergence on the primary unit-edge graph. For a zero-total profile, the minimizing gradient flow gives the inverse-Laplacian cost; the divergence-free part is removed by orthogonal decomposition. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Two-way restoration transfer concerns probabilities of random guard data under cut and restored equilibrium laws. It does not construct a communication protocol, distributed clock model, or synchronization dynamics. |
| other (Mathematical physics and statistical mechanics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | For a finite free spin rectangle, the paper constructs an exact dual representation of the spin correlation as a twisted-to-centered height partition ratio. Fourier currents become face-height differences, while a source path supplies the integral connection. This is a concrete analytical representation; its height sums remain unbounded. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-spin-model-and-boundaries** (formal_scope): The critical exponent concerns the ordinary nearest-neighbor cosine interaction on the square lattice with the stated free spin boundaries. Blocked use: ['Transfer the critical exponent to other interactions, lattice geometries, fixed boundary spins, external fields, or boundary identifications without a separate theorem.']
- **limit-volume-before-separation** (hypothesis): The free-box infinite-volume limit is taken at each fixed separation before the separation limit. Blocked use: ['Replace the prescribed iterated limit with a simultaneous volume and separation limit.', 'Certify the infinite-volume exponent from a fixed finite box.']
- **limit-exponent-without-amplitude** (unknown): The exponent theorem does not establish convergence of the correlation after multiplication by the critical power or identify a multiplicative logarithmic correction. Blocked use: ['Claim a limiting critical amplitude.', 'Infer a particular multiplicative logarithmic factor from the exponent limit.']
- **limit-critical-versus-positive-coefficient-regime** (formal_scope): The critical exponent is identified at the mass-defined threshold. Away from that threshold, the positive-coefficient spin bounds depend on the effective height coefficient. Blocked use: ['Assign the critical exponent throughout the entire massless regime.', 'Use the magnetic lower bound when the height coefficient vanishes.']
- **limit-affine-and-tilted-comparisons** (hypothesis): Finite comparisons require the specified nonnegative precision and admissible affine supports. Numerator-only real tilts preserve ratio monotonicity but can invalidate the bound by one. Blocked use: ['Bound a real-tilted shifted-to-centered ratio by one.', 'Insert an equality constraint with an empty affine constraint set.', 'Apply centered precision comparisons to an indefinite added form.']
- **limit-primary-cost-not-bare-chain-variance** (formal_scope): Subdivision-uniform moment control uses primary unit-edge flow costs and whole-chain increments, rather than the inverse microscopic Gaussian precision. Blocked use: ['Use bare chain resistance as a subdivision-uniform primary variance bound.', 'Assume a uniform microscopic Gaussian tail for the original Bessel law.']
- **limit-free-smear-admissibility-and-degeneracy** (hypothesis): Free Gaussian limits require the stated admissible rectangle sequences and smooth shifts or convergent mean-free density profiles. The limiting field may be degenerate. Blocked use: ['Extend the free smear theorem to arbitrary singular profiles without reference-energy approximation.', 'Assert a nondegenerate Gaussian limit solely from existence of the free coefficient.']
- **limit-mixed-geometry-and-primary-pins** (hypothesis): Mixed limits use fixed face-connected rectangular geometries and positive-area primary pin regions. Point contacts do not identify components, and subdivision vertices are not included in the primary hard pin. Blocked use: ['Glue components through point contacts or opposite-only vertex junctions.', 'Replace a primary region pin with a sparse site pin or an internal-chain pin without proving the required transfer.']
- **limit-component-constant-control** (hypothesis): Unpinned components require mean-free tests unless confining observations control their constants. Translation copies are summed before those constants are integrated in the Gaussian limit. Blocked use: ['Treat an uncontrolled component constant as an ordinary integrable Gaussian coordinate.', 'Fix component constants silently when using the unprojected confining-law limit.']
- **limit-band-refinement-order** (hypothesis): Band arguments refine the primary mesh, then the band cells, and only afterwards increase the band strength. Weighted moment and sign-resampling assertions retain their stated fixed-parameter regimes. Blocked use: ['Interchange band strength and mesh limits.', 'Interpret an iterated limiting moment bound as a uniform lattice quadratic-exponential estimate.']
- **limit-exploration-centered-posterior** (formal_scope): Path anchoring bounds centered unvisited posterior observations after macroscopic exposure. Actual exposed values generate a linear tilt that requires separate control. Blocked use: ['Treat visited exposed heights as zero in the actual sampled configuration.', 'Apply centered posterior bounds directly to an uncontrolled large posterior tilt.', 'Replace the polynomial damping and truncation stages with an assumed Gaussian tail.']
- **limit-conditional-typical-data** (approximation): Conditional convergence is through finite-observation approximations in probability at typical guard data. The probe list is increased after the primary lattice limit. Blocked use: ['Claim uniform convergence over all microscopic guard configurations.', 'Infer a pointwise continuum trace from the finite-probe convergence theorem.']
- **limit-centered-ratios-under-tilted-sampling** (formal_scope): Pin interactions and normalized pin ratios retain their centered definitions under fixed linear-tilt sampling. The centered expectation identity does not become an identity under the tilted measure. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the centered pin-ratio expectation identity under tilted sampling.', "Redefine the centered interaction while invoking the theorem's tilted-data convergence."]
- **limit-restoration-support-containment** (hypothesis): The hard core pin must include both primary endpoints of every restored bond and the entire support of every restored positive form, with separation from the guard. Blocked use: ['Transfer negligible guard-data events across a restoration whose support crosses the core or guard.', 'Require internal chain vertices to be pinned as a substitute for checking primary support containment.']
- **limit-winding-sector-normalization** (formal_scope): Convergence of ordinary height observations does not determine a winding-sector partition ratio. The magnetic lower bound also requires guarded isolation, factorization, rim tightness, and hole filling. Blocked use: ['Derive the magnetic partition ratio solely from unconditioned Gaussian smear limits.', 'Discard accumulated annular errors before fixing localization and microscopic hole parameters.']
- **limit-strengthened-law-and-fixed-reference** (formal_scope): The openness construction adds precision on block averages and uses a fixed gapped Gaussian reference. Its observed large-field reserve is a property of the strengthened law; the added precision is removed by comparison before concluding positivity for the original model. Blocked use: ['Attribute the strengthened observed-field Gaussian reserve to bare microscopic Bessel heights.', 'Apply supermarginal openness at the marginal coefficient.', 'Assume continuity of the original effective coefficient.']
- **limit-full-analytic-entry** (hypothesis): Entry requires the full analytic field-derivative norm, large-field regulator reserve, compact-data refinements, and compatible torus copying. Agreement on bounded real observations alone is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Certify analytic entry from bounded-real-data convergence alone.', 'Extend the exact torus representation to incompatible sides without checking its geometry and copying hypotheses.']
- **limit-no-finite-certificate-or-selection-modulus** (nonconstructive): The principal conclusions involve infinite-volume and refinement limits. The entry theorem asserts existence of scales and an interval but does not supply a finite selection procedure or a certified convergence modulus. Blocked use: ['Claim that finite test vectors decide the critical exponent theorem.', 'Assign an algorithmic runtime to selecting the entry scales or the eventual separation threshold.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For the ordinary nearest-neighbor XY model on the square lattice, we prove the Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless prediction that the critical spin-correlation exponent is 1/4. More precisely, at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature, the infinite-volume correlation is $`n^{-1/4+o(1)}`$. The infinite-volume limit through free square boxes is taken before the separation limit.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

The two-dimensional XY model has an algebraic phase even though its spins have no preferred direction. The Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless theory predicts that the correlation exponent at the boundary of that phase is exactly $1/4$ <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Berezinskii](#ref-Berezinskii), [BKT1973](#ref-BKT1973), [Kosterlitz1974](#ref-Kosterlitz1974)]. This paper proves that prediction for the nearest-neighbor cosine interaction on the square lattice, at the threshold defined by vanishing horizontal mass. The conclusion concerns free-box infinite-volume correlations at all integer separations.

Several earlier results establish the surrounding phase picture. McBryan and Spencer proved power-law upper bounds for planar spin correlations <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[McBryanSpencer1977](#ref-McBryanSpencer1977)]. Fröhlich and Spencer proved the existence of the low-temperature algebraic phase by a Coulomb-gas analysis <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FS](#ref-FS)]. Van Engelenburg and Lis obtained the exponential-versus-polynomial dichotomy, including a polynomial lower bound at the threshold, by directed currents and dual heights <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[vEL](#ref-vEL)]. Their critical lower bound is the external endpoint input used here; its exponent is not sharp.

The dual-height approach also has a substantial independent history. Aizenman, Harel, Peled, and Shapiro developed integer-restricted Gaussian and annealed Gaussian methods for depinning and BKT phases, including a Gaussian-mixture representation of Bessel weights <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AHPS2022](#ref-AHPS2022)]. Lammers identified the exact relation between XY mass and its dual height mass <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[LammersBijecting2023](#ref-LammersBijecting2023)], and proved a qualitative localization dichotomy with a positive universal height-variance gap <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[LammersDichotomy2026](#ref-LammersDichotomy2026)]. The broader duality identities of van Engelenburg and Lis <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[vanEngelenburgLisDuality2025](#ref-vanEngelenburgLisDuality2025)] and the surface-tension derivative formula of Durand and Lammers <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DurandLammers2026](#ref-DurandLammers2026)] further connect spin decay and height fluctuations. These results do not identify the endpoint coefficient or its magnetic correlation exponent. Those two identifications are the tasks addressed below.

Our companion article <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BKT](#ref-BKT)] supplies the finite Gaussian comparisons, annular geometry and Gaussian estimates, and local analytic maps used in the proof. Its discrete-height theorems concern quadratic weights, and its spin theorem concerns sufficiently low fixed temperatures. We therefore prove the model-specific transfers to the Bessel interaction in this paper. Every companion citation gives a numbered statement or equation. Section <a href="#sec:interfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:interfaces">1.3</a> specifies the hypotheses at these interfaces.

Three extensions are particularly important. First, a subdivision by lattice Gaussian chains transfers finite comparisons to Bessel weights, but not their bare Gaussian variance bound. Unit-edge flow costs and arbitrarily high polynomial moments replace that bound, including after cable exploration and for very small pin probabilities. Second, convergence of ordinary height observations does not determine a winding-sector partition ratio. A finite isolation expansion on geometric annuli supplies that comparison, with only a small error per annular scale. Third, an additional positive form on *averages*, rather than on microscopic heights, supplies the large-field reserve needed for an open Gaussian regime. This form is removed by a variance comparison before drawing a conclusion about the original model.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Model and statement

<a id="eq:xy-law"></a>
<a id="eq:correlation"></a>
<a id="eq:critical-definition"></a>
For an integer $L\ge1$, put $$\Lambda_L=[-L,L]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2,
 \qquad
 E_L=\{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda_L:|x-y|=1\}.$$ Every edge occurs once. At inverse temperature $b>0$, the angles $\theta_x\in\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z)$ have law $$\label{eq:xy-law}
 \,\mathrm d\mu_{L,b}(\theta)
 =Z_{L,b}^{-1}
   \exp\!\left\{b\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E_L}
                 \cos(\theta_x-\theta_y)\right\}
   \prod_{x\in\Lambda_L}\frac{\,\mathrm d\theta_x}{2\pi}.$$ There are no exterior edges, fixed spins, fields, or boundary identifications. With $e_1=(1,0)$, set $$\label{eq:correlation}
 C_b(n)=\lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\L\ge n}}
  \mathbb E_{\mu_{L,b}}\cos(\theta_0-\theta_{ne_1}),\qquad
 m(b)=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac1n\log C_b(n),$$ and $$\label{eq:critical-definition}
 b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}.$$ The correlation limit exists by ferromagnetic coupling monotonicity <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Ginibre1970](#ref-Ginibre1970)]. The multiplicative correlation inequality <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[vEL](#ref-vEL), arXiv version 2, Lemma 19], after free exhaustion and translation invariance, gives $C_b(n+m)\ge C_b(n)C_b(m)$. Fekete’s lemma therefore gives the mass limit. The dichotomy in <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[vEL](#ref-vEL), Theorem 1] implies $0<b_c<\infty$ and includes $b_c$ in the massless regime. The parameter $b$ is exactly the $\beta$ of the usual cosine convention. Throughout the proof, the first limit in <a href="#eq:correlation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:correlation">[eq:correlation]</a> is taken before the separation limit.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For the model <a href="#eq:xy-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:xy-law">[eq:xy-law]</a> and the threshold <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>, $$\lim_{\substack{n\to\infty\\n\in\mathbb N,\ n\ge2}}
       \frac{\log C_{b_c}(n)}{\log n}=-\frac14.$$ Equivalently, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $n_0<\infty$ such that $$n^{-1/4-\varepsilon}\le C_{b_c}(n)\le n^{-1/4+\varepsilon}
 \qquad(n\ge n_0).$$*

</div>

We use the following established, nonsharp critical input.

<a id="stmt-prop-critical-lower"></a>
<div id="prop:critical-lower" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Critical polynomial lower bound). *For every $n\ge1$, $$C_{b_c}(n)\ge\frac1{8n}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-critical-lower"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-critical-lower</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem 1 of van Engelenburg and Lis <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[vEL](#ref-vEL)] is stated for free-boundary XY spins with density $\exp\{\frac b2\sum_{\{x,y\}}
(\sigma_x\overline{\sigma_y}+
 \overline{\sigma_x}\sigma_y)\}$. Writing $\sigma_x=e^{i\theta_x}$ gives exactly <a href="#eq:xy-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:xy-law">[eq:xy-law]</a>. Their infinite-volume correlation is the free finite-graph exhaustion, hence agrees with <a href="#eq:correlation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:correlation">[eq:correlation]</a>; reflection of all angles removes its imaginary part. Their theorem gives exponential decay below a threshold and the lower bound $1/(8|x-y|)$ at and above it. These alternatives have positive and zero horizontal mass, respectively, so their threshold is <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>. Specialize to $x=0,y=ne_1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Structure of the proof

Section <a href="#sec:heights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:heights">2</a> constructs the free height coefficient $a=a(b)$, proves $a\in\{0\}\cup[8\pi,\infty)$, and gives the spin upper exponent $-2\pi/a$, with arbitrarily fast polynomial decay when $a=0$. Section <a href="#sec:pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pins">3</a> proves the mixed-boundary and conditional pin limits needed for localization. Section <a href="#sec:magnetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:magnetic">4</a> proves the matching lower exponent whenever $a>0$, and thus zero spin mass in that case. Section <a href="#sec:openness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:openness">5</a> shows that $a(b)>8\pi$ implies positivity of the coefficient in a neighborhood of $b$. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:critical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:critical">6</a> combines these facts with Proposition <a href="#prop:critical-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:critical-lower">2</a> to identify $a(b_c)=8\pi$. No continuity of the unknown function $a(b)$ is assumed.

The constants can be followed through a short chain of identities. The height period is $2\pi$, and a radial unit flow has energy $(2\pi)^{-1}\log r+O(1)$. Summing its Fourier sectors yields the gap $a\ge8\pi$ whenever $a>0$. A pair of opposite angular singularities has energy $4\pi\log n+O(1)$, giving spin exponent $2\pi/a$. The critical lower bound excludes $a(b_c)=0$, while openness excludes $a(b_c)>8\pi$. Thus $2\pi/a(b_c)=1/4$.

<div id="tab:conventions">

| Convention or limit      | Meaning in this paper                                                                                                                                                       |
|:-------------------------|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Spin coupling            | Each unoriented nearest-neighbor edge appears once in $b\cos(\theta_x-\theta_y)$.                                                                                           |
| Dual height              | $h\in2\pi\mathbb Z$, with normalized edge weights $p_b(j)=e^{-b}I_j(b)$. The ordinary unit-edge Laplacian is $A$.                                                           |
| Effective coefficient    | Height covariance is $aA^{-1}$; the corresponding Gaussian energy is $\langle h,Ah\rangle/(2a)$.                                                                            |
| Thermodynamic limit      | First $L\to\infty$ at each fixed integer $n$; then $n\to\infty$, including nondyadic separations.                                                                           |
| Subdivision              | The chain length tends to infinity on each fixed primary graph before a macroscopic geometry is refined.                                                                    |
| Bands and magnetic holes | Bands use mesh refinement, then band-cell refinement, then increasing band strength. Magnetic hole size and localization parameters are fixed through the separation limit. |

Normalizations and the order of limits. A Gaussian comparison at an auxiliary scale does not change the bare XY coupling.

</div>

<a id="sec:interfaces"></a>
#### Companion inputs and Bessel transfers

The quadratic companion is used at three distinct levels. First, BKTref@st:finite-gaussian

applies on finite-dimensional lattices with positive quadratic precision. In Lemma <a href="#lem:comparisons" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:comparisons">4</a> we put the Bessel law on finite Gaussian-chain approximants and pass to the limit with every primary graph fixed. Affine supports, centered constraints, observation penalties, and numerator-only real tilts are checked there. Lemma <a href="#lem:reference-cost" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:reference-cost">5</a> supplies uniform moment bounds in the ordinary unit-edge flow norm. The bare inverse precision of a subdivided chain is not used as a uniform bound.

Second, BKTref@an:forms

and BKTref@an:gaussian-localization

concern fixed face-connected rectangular geometries, ordinary real Gaussian forms, observation-cell masses, and separated pins. These geometric and Gaussian conclusions apply with the coefficient $a(b)>0$. The Bessel mixed-boundary and conditional statements are instead proved in Theorems <a href="#thm:mixed-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:mixed-limits">9</a> and <a href="#thm:pin-interaction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:pin-interaction">10</a>: primary path bounds, polynomial cluster moments, and a relative small-tilt estimate replace the quadratic tail argument. Full conditional limits refer to typical guard data through finite probes; they are not uniform limits at every microscopic boundary value. Lemma <a href="#lem:restoration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:restoration">17</a> checks that all restored supports lie in the pinned core.

Third, the analytic map BKTref@wh:map

acts on small real, even, periodic activities with block and square covariance, in the full analytic norm with enlarged support collars and regulator reserve. Lemma <a href="#lem:op-entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:op-entry">24</a> verifies every such condition for the strengthened Bessel law, at a fixed gapped Gaussian reference $a'>8\pi$. Its entry proof is given here; the companion’s quadratic physical entry theorem is not an assumption about Bessel weights. The subsequent Gaussian-curve argument uses a common small disk for sufficiently large starting scales, with the reference frequency held fixed. Finally we remove the added precision by comparison. This gives the openness needed at criticality without any continuity assumption on $a(b)$.

<a id="sec:heights"></a>
### Dual heights and the structural coefficient

Throughout the proof, $b>0$ denotes the spin inverse temperature. The height spacing is $2\pi$. On an original, or *primary*, dual edge the weight of the difference $2\pi j$ is $$p_b(j)=e^{-b}I_j(b),\qquad
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}p_b(j)e^{tj}=\exp\{b(\cosh t-1)\}.$$ Thus $p_b$ is the law of the difference of two independent Poisson variables of mean $b/2$. An unoriented edge is counted once. For a retained primary graph, $A$ is its unit-conductance Laplacian: $$\langle g,Ag\rangle=\sum_{\{x,y\}}(g_x-g_y)^2.$$ This normalization converges to $\int|\nabla g|^2$. Pairings on a finite graph use counting measure. A free connected height component is taken modulo common translations by $2\pi\mathbb Z$; one may choose a pinned representative when evaluating translation-invariant tests. When confining observations control the constant, all its translation copies are instead summed.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Duality and finite comparisons

The current-to-height correspondence below is the standard planar XY duality; see <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[vEL](#ref-vEL), Section 2] and <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[vanEngelenburgLisDuality2025](#ref-vanEngelenburgLisDuality2025)]. We give the finite free-box identity to fix its boundary and winding conventions.

<a id="stmt-lem-duality"></a>
<div id="lem:duality" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Free-boundary duality). *In a finite free spin rectangle, the two-point spin correlation is the ratio of a twisted height partition sum to the centered height partition sum, both with weights $p_b$ and exterior face height zero. The twist is an integral connection with circulations $+2\pi,-2\pi$ around the two source vertices and zero circulation elsewhere. For interior sources it can vanish near the outer boundary. After cuts, the same description holds on each retained chart, without identifying heights through the cut.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-duality"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-duality</summary>

*Proof.* Expand each factor $e^{b\cos(\theta_x-\theta_y)}$ in its Fourier series. Integrating the spins imposes zero divergence on the integer edge current in the denominator. Inserting $e^{i(\theta_0-\theta_x)}$ changes its divergence to the two unit sources. The insertion with the opposite sign has the same sum by current reversal, so taking the real part makes no change. The factors $e^b$ cancel between the two partition sums.

Rotate currents onto dual edges. In a planar rectangle, every divergence-free integral current is a difference of integer face heights with exterior height zero. This follows by summing the rotated current along dual paths: path independence is exactly the zero-divergence condition at the enclosed primal vertices. Subtract a fixed unit current on a path between the sources in the numerator. The remaining current is divergence free and gives the same height variables. The subtracted current is the claimed connection. Changing its path is an integral change of height variables. Equivalently, the numerator has multivalued heights with the stated monodromies and single-valued branches on simply connected charts. Releasing identifications across a cut gives the asserted cut version. ◻

</details>

The finite Gaussian inequalities of BKTref@st:finite-gaussian

, based on the lattice inequality of Regev and Stephens-Davidowitz <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[RSD](#ref-RSD)], apply to this nonquadratic weight through a series approximation. We record the extension, including the forms needed after conditioning. The general use of Gaussian representations for this transfer has precedent in <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AHPS2022](#ref-AHPS2022), Lemmas 9.6–9.7 and Appendices C–D]; the Bessel mixture representation there is credited to Aran Raoufi. The series construction below verifies the particular affine constraints and observation forms used here. It does not require the bare Bessel potential to satisfy a super-Gaussian condition at every value of $b$; subdivision is used in such extensions, as also in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[LammersDichotomy2026](#ref-LammersDichotomy2026), Theorem 8 and Section 1.3.3].

<a id="stmt-lem-comparisons"></a>
<div id="lem:comparisons" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Finite comparison rules). *On a fixed primary graph, allow additional nonnegative quadratic forms in real linear observations, centered equalities, and independent Gaussian observation noises.*

1.  *Increasing centered precision decreases centered covariance and every centered real moment generating function. Covariance on a translate, including after a real linear tilt, dominates centered covariance. In particular $$\log\mathbb Ee^X\ge \tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)$$ for a centered inversion-symmetric law and a real linear test $X$.*

2.  *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same positive precision is added to the affine and centered coordinates. Shifts may be nonzero equality values, an integral connection, or centers of observation penalties. The monotonicity also holds with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent, but the bound by one need not hold for this tilted ratio. Equality constraints may be inserted by infinite penalties whenever the affine constraint is nonempty.*

3.  *Centered characteristic coefficients are nonnegative and increase with precision. Their logarithms have Hessian at every phase at least the Hessian at zero.
